</section>
<section id="S4.SS0.SSS0.Px2" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h5 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Model weights.</h5>

<div id="S4.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="S4.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We release the <span id="S4.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> and <span id="S4.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Fast</span> weights under the TABPFN-3.5 License v1.0, designed to be permissive for academic use, research, and evaluation in commercial settings. The license explicitly allows testing, evaluation, and internal benchmarking, so an organization can download the models and run preliminary assessments on its own datasets without a commercial agreement. The key restriction is that the models, their derivatives, and their outputs cannot be used for commercial or production purposes. This includes, but is not limited to, revenue-generating products, competitive benchmarking for procurement decisions, client deliverables, and using model outputs as inputs to internal commercial decision-making. The full TABPFN-3.5 License v1.0 text is available at <a href="https://huggingface.co/Prior-Labs/tabpfn_3_5/blob/main/LICENSE" title="" class="ltx_ref ltx_url ltx_font_typewriter">https://huggingface.co/Prior-Labs/tabpfn_3_5/blob/main/LICENSE</a>.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="S4.SS0.SSS0.Px3" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h5 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Commercial use.</h5>

<div id="S4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="S4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">For production use, we offer a Commercial Enterprise License, available for our managed API, SAP AI Core, Virtual Private Cloud deployments (at the time of publication: AWS SageMaker &amp; Azure AI Foundry), and on-prem or other custom deployment modes across other software platforms such as Databricks. The Commercial Enterprise License provides access to our proprietary high-speed inference engine, dedicated support, integration tooling, additional internal models, and the <span id="S4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Plus</span> and <span id="S4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Thinking</span> variants, which are not available as part of the open-source release. The managed API runs on our optimized GPU infrastructure and is the recommended option for users without dedicated local GPUs; it is accessible via the Python client SDK (<span id="S4.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">pip install tabpfn-client</span>) or a standard REST API. For commercial licensing inquiries, please contact <a href="mailto:sales@priorlabs.ai" title="" class="ltx_ref ltx_href">sales@priorlabs.ai</a>.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="S4.SS1" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">4.1 </span>Distribution</h3>

<div id="S4.SS1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="S4.SS1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.SS1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> and <span id="S4.SS1.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Fast</span> are available through the open-source PyPI distribution for evaluation and non-commercial use, and through a managed API for commercial workloads. The models are listed on the AWS SageMaker Marketplace<span id="footnote3" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">3</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">3</sup>
              <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">3</span>
              
              
              
              
              
              
              
            <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/marketplace/pp/prodview-chfhncrdzlb3s" title="" class="ltx_ref ltx_url ltx_font_typewriter">https://aws.amazon.com/marketplace/pp/prodview-chfhncrdzlb3s</a></span></span></span>, the Azure AI Foundry Model Catalog<span id="footnote4" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">4</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">4</sup>
              <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">4</span>
              
              
              
              
              
              
              
            <a href="https://ai.azure.com/catalog/models/TabPFN-2.5" title="" class="ltx_ref ltx_url ltx_font_typewriter">https://ai.azure.com/catalog/models/TabPFN-2.5</a></span></span></span> and SAP AI Core with full support for batch and real-time inference on classification and regression tasks; <span id="S4.SS1.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> will become available on these marketplaces with this report. A reference integration for Databricks is available through the Databricks Industry Solutions repository<span id="footnote5" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">5</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">5</sup>
              <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">5</span>

<section id="A3" class="ltx_appendix">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_appendix"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix C </span>More Experimental Results</h2>

<section id="A3.SS1" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">C.1 </span>Additional details on TabArena</h3>

<section id="A3.SS1.SSS1" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsubsection">C.1.1 </span>Tuning trajectories and pairwise win rates on TabArena</h4>

<div id="A3.SS1.SSS1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS1.SSS1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><a href="#A3.F10" title="In C.1.1 Tuning trajectories and pairwise win rates on TabArena ‣ C.1 Additional details on TabArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">10</span></a> places the <span id="A3.SS1.SSS1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> family on TabArena’s
improvability-against-time plane together with the tuning trajectories of the classical
baselines: each connected path shows a model’s tuned-and-ensembled portfolio as the number of
random configurations grows from one to all, left to right, with the default configuration as
the left-most point. Improvability is how much a model would gain by switching to the best
model on each dataset. <a href="#A3.F10" title="In C.1.1 Tuning trajectories and pairwise win rates on TabArena ‣ C.1 Additional details on TabArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">10</span></a> gives the pairwise win rates of the same
methods over the 816 tasks. <a href="#A3.F12" title="In C.1.1 Tuning trajectories and pairwise win rates on TabArena ‣ C.1 Additional details on TabArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figures</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">12</span></a> and <a href="#A3.F12" title="Figure 12 ‣ C.1.1 Tuning trajectories and pairwise win rates on TabArena ‣ C.1 Additional details on TabArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">12</span></a>
repeat both views on TabArena-medium, the 15 datasets with 10k–100k training rows.</p>
</div>

<section id="A3.SS1.SSS2" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsubsection">C.1.2 </span>TabArena leaderboard tables</h4>

<div id="A3.SS1.SSS2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS1.SSS2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We present the leaderboard tables for
<a href="#S1.T2" title="Table 2 ‣ C.1.2 TabArena leaderboard tables ‣ C.1 Additional details on TabArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref">TabArena</a>,
<a href="#A3.T3" title="Table 3 ‣ C.1.2 TabArena leaderboard tables ‣ C.1 Additional details on TabArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref">TabArena-small</a>,
<a href="#A3.T4" title="Table 4 ‣ C.1.2 TabArena leaderboard tables ‣ C.1 Additional details on TabArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref">TabArena-medium</a>,
<a href="#A3.T5" title="Table 5 ‣ C.1.2 TabArena leaderboard tables ‣ C.1 Additional details on TabArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref">TabArena-classification</a>, and
<a href="#A3.T6" title="Table 6 ‣ C.1.2 TabArena leaderboard tables ‣ C.1 Additional details on TabArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref">TabArena-regression</a> below. Elo, win counts and
improvability are computed over the full TabArena field; the tables show the methods of the
report’s figures. Train and predict times are medians over the datasets of each view of the
time per 1,000 rows, measured as described in <a href="#A3.SS1.SSS4" title="C.1.4 How the time axis of the TabArena Pareto frontier is computed ‣ C.1 Additional details on TabArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">C.1.4</span></a>.</p>
</div>
<div id="A3.SS1.SSS2.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS1.SSS2.p2.1" class="ltx_p">In all five views, <span id="A3.SS1.SSS2.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Thinking</span> ranks first in Elo. <span id="A3.SS1.SSS2.p2.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> ranks second in every
view except TabArena-small, where TabFM+ places between the two, and <span id="A3.SS1.SSS2.p2.1.3" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Fast</span> reaches
the Elo of TabFM on the full benchmark at a fraction of its train and predict time.</p>
</div>

<section id="A3.SS1.SSS3" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsubsection">C.1.3 </span>Additional results on TabArena</h4>

<section id="A3.SS1.SSS3.Px1" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h5 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">What drives improvability differences?</h5>

<div id="A3.SS1.SSS3.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS1.SSS3.Px1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><a href="#A3.F13" title="In What drives improvability differences? ‣ C.1.3 Additional results on TabArena ‣ C.1 Additional details on TabArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">13</span></a> shows the improvability as a function of total fit-and-predict time.
Although TabFM and TabFM+ achieve slightly higher total improvability than <span id="A3.SS1.SSS3.Px1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span>, the per-dataset comparison in <a href="#A3.F14" title="In What drives improvability differences? ‣ C.1.3 Additional results on TabArena ‣ C.1 Additional details on TabArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">fig.</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">14</span></a> shows that the remaining gap is dominated by two datasets: <span id="A3.SS1.SSS3.Px1.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">hazelnut-spread-contaminant-detection</span> and <span id="A3.SS1.SSS3.Px1.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">polish-companies-bankruptcy</span>. Both are already nearly perfectly solved, with ROC AUC above 0.997, which leads to improvability inflating the small absolute residual differences.</p>
</div>
<figure id="A3.F13" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="2609.17895v2/pareto_front_improvability_vs_time_total_joint_setC__20260909-tabarena-rtxpro6000-wrapperfix.svg" id="A3.F13.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" style="aspect-ratio:476/279;" width="476" height="279"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A3.F13.4" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 13</span>: </span><span id="A3.F13.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" style="font-size:90%;">TabArena improvability against total time.<span id="A3.F13.5.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_medium"> Lower is better. Same methods and time axis as <a href="#S2.F2" title="In 2.1 TabArena ‣ 2 Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>; <a href="#A3.SS1.SSS4" title="C.1.4 How the time axis of the TabArena Pareto frontier is computed ‣ C.1 Additional details on TabArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">C.1.4</span></a> describes the measurement.</span></span></figcaption>
</figure>
<figure id="A3.F14" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="2609.17895v2/tabarena-improvability-gap-vs-tabfm-plus__20260904-tabarena-41d05a3c4b.svg" id="A3.F14.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" style="aspect-ratio:476/219;" width="476" height="219"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A3.F14.5" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 14</span>: </span><span id="A3.F14.6" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Improvability vs. TabFM+, we can see that the improvability gap in the final Pareto Frontier boils down to <span id="A3.F14.6.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">hazelnut-spread-contaminant-detection</span> and <span id="A3.F14.6.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">polish-companies-bankruptcy</span>, both of which are almost perfectly solved, with ROC AUC exceeding 0.997.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
</section>
<section id="A3.SS1.SSS4" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsubsection">C.1.4 </span>How the time axis of the TabArena Pareto frontier is computed</h4>

<section id="A3.SS1.SSS4.Px1" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h5 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Protocol.</h5>

<div id="A3.SS1.SSS4.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS1.SSS4.Px1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">All times in <a href="#S2.F2" title="In 2.1 TabArena ‣ 2 Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a> follow TabArena’s default protocol <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib2" title="" class="ltx_ref">2</a>]</cite>: every method is fitted as an 8-fold bagging ensemble with a final refit on the full training set, in a fresh process per split, on one node with 24 CPU cores and one NVIDIA RTX Pro 6000 GPU. The fit time of a split is the wall-clock time of the whole bagged fit; the prediction time is the wall-clock time of the refitted model predicting the test third of the dataset. While the 8-fold bagging is not used for TFMs in general, as they are refitted on the full training set during inference, we keep it to make the comparison fairer to other non-TFM models, which could also be fitted without bagging (although with weaker performance). Models which are not default or tuning configurations of foundation models, neural networks or trees (<span id="A3.SS1.SSS4.Px1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Thinking</span>, <span id="A3.SS1.SSS4.Px1.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span>-Thinking and systems like TabFM+ and AutoGluon 1.6) are instead called once per split, so their fit time might look a bit better than that of the bagged configurations; this is why TabFM+ shows a lower total time than TabFM in <a href="#S2.F2" title="In 2.1 TabArena ‣ 2 Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="A3.SS1.SSS4.Px2" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h5 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Normalization.</h5>

<div id="A3.SS1.SSS4.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS1.SSS4.Px2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Per split, fit time is expressed per 1,000 training rows and prediction time per 1,000 test rows, averaged over folds per dataset and reported as the median over the 51 datasets, as in the TabArena leaderboard <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib2" title="" class="ltx_ref">2</a>]</cite>. The total time of a split is the sum of the two per-1,000-row rates, the definition TabArena’s code uses for its combined time axis.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="A3.SS1.SSS4.Px3" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h5 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Re-timing of the foundation models.</h5>

<div id="A3.SS1.SSS4.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS1.SSS4.Px3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">The arena’s wrappers for tabular foundation models carried overheads that are not part of running the model and were often larger than actual train times for small datasets, making it hard to compare different TFMs’ speeds: the network was rebuilt from its checkpoint and copied to the GPU for each of the nine bagging children, the child models were saved with the full network weights inside, and the library import and the first forward pass of the process fell inside the timed fit. We removed these in the wrappers (one network per process shared across the children, no weights in the saved children, and an untimed warm-up on fixed synthetic data) and re-timed <span id="A3.SS1.SSS4.Px3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span>, <span id="A3.SS1.SSS4.Px3.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Fast</span>, TabFM, TabFM+, EXAONE-Tabular, <span id="A3.SS1.SSS4.Px3.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span>, TabICLv2, TabDPT-Turbo <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib35" title="" class="ltx_ref">35</a>]</cite> and TabSwift on the first repeat of every dataset (3 folds, 153 splits), with the Python environment on the node’s local disk. Predictions are unchanged, so the Elo scores are those of the full 816-split evaluations. The tree-based and neural baselines use the arena’s own measurements. <span id="A3.SS1.SSS4.Px3.p1.1.4" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span>-Thinking keeps the Elo and time of its published run from May 2026, which predates these fixes, so its time is an upper bound.</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>
</section>
<section id="A3.SS2" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">C.2 </span>Additional results on BeyondArena</h3>

<section id="A3.SS2.SSS1" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsubsection">C.2.1 </span>Radar view of the BeyondArena slices</h4>

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS2.SSS1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><a href="#A3.F15" title="In C.2.1 Radar view of the BeyondArena slices ‣ C.2 Additional results on BeyondArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">15</span></a> shows the Elo scores of <a href="#S2.F3" title="In 2.2 BeyondArena ‣ 2 Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a> and <a href="#S2.F4" title="In 2.2 BeyondArena ‣ 2 Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a> as radar charts, with one axis per data slice. The numbers are the same as in the bar charts.</p>
</div>

</section>
<section id="A3.SS2.SSS2" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsubsection">C.2.2 </span>Comparison to recent TFMs</h4>

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS2.SSS2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We additionally compare <span id="A3.SS2.SSS2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> with TabFM <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib33" title="" class="ltx_ref">33</a>]</cite> and EXAONE-Tabular <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib34" title="" class="ltx_ref">34</a>]</cite> on the subset of BeyondArena that both models can run (<a href="#A3.F16" title="In C.2.2 Comparison to recent TFMs ‣ C.2 Additional results on BeyondArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">16</span></a>). The subset is BeyondArena core without large tables (up to 100K rows), minus five datasets: <span id="A3.SS2.SSS2.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">asp_potassco_classification</span>, <span id="A3.SS2.SSS2.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">ljubljana_primary_tumor</span> and <span id="A3.SS2.SSS2.p1.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">micro_mass</span> have more than ten classes, which TabFM does not support, and <span id="A3.SS2.SSS2.p1.1.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">sf_permit_time</span> (TabFM and EXAONE-Tabular) and <span id="A3.SS2.SSS2.p1.1.6" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">pva_revenue_prediction_kddcup98</span> (TabFM) run out of memory on a 96 GB GPU. This leaves 116 datasets, on which every method is scored on the same splits. <span id="A3.SS2.SSS2.p1.1.7" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> leads both models overall and across every data slice except small datasets, where TabFM leads. Again, wide confidence intervals make some slice-level differences uncertain.</p>
</div>
<figure id="A3.F16" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="2609.17895v2/beyondarena-tfm-splits-per-method__20260909-beyondarena-3way-116ds-gid.svg" id="A3.F16.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" style="aspect-ratio:476/143;" width="476" height="143"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A3.F16.6" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 16</span>: </span><span id="A3.F16.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" style="font-size:90%;">Comparison with recent tabular foundation models on BeyondArena.<span id="A3.F16.7.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_medium"> Elo scores on the 116 BeyondArena core datasets without large tables that <span id="A3.F16.7.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span>, TabFM, and EXAONE-Tabular all support (see text), reported overall and by data slice. <span id="A3.F16.7.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> leads overall and across nearly every slice. The group identifier is exposed to the TabPFN models and to TabFM on label-per-sample grouped datasets, not to EXAONE-Tabular; see <a href="#A3.SS2.SSS3" title="C.2.3 Exposing the group identifier on label-per-sample grouped tasks ‣ C.2 Additional results on BeyondArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">C.2.3</span></a>. The labels of the Elo scores are rounded to the nearest multiple of 10.</span></span></figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
<section id="A3.SS2.SSS3" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsubsection">C.2.3 </span>Exposing the group identifier on label-per-sample grouped tasks</h4>

<div id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">On grouped datasets, BeyondArena applies a shared preprocessing step which drops the group id before the table reaches a model.
On <span id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">label-per-group</span> tasks where every row of a group shares its label, BeyondArena also adds transductive feature aggregates within each group, which still encode which group a row belongs to.
For <span id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">label-per-sample</span> tasks, where labels vary within a group, no other grouped-specific preprocessing is done besides dropping the group id, so the group structure is not passed to the model.
Not every method is affected in the same way: tuned and early-stopped baselines still exploit the group structure, because their validation splits are group-aware, whereas models which are fit on the full training set
(TFMs) cannot exploit the group structure.</p>
</div>
<div id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p2.1" class="ltx_p">To make the comparison fairer and closer to what practitioners can use in practice, our aim on label-per-sample tasks is to score every model in the configuration that serves it best. We therefore tried exposing the
group key to every model family with several encodings (float, categorical, string), passing it raw or marking unseen test groups as missing. Simply passing the group id as a categorical variable helps every TabPFN model
and we score <span id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span>, <span id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p2.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Fast</span>, <span id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p2.1.3" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Thinking</span> and <span id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p2.1.4" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span> with it. We score TabFM with the group id exposed as a float, unseen test groups marked as missing, which we find helps the most for this
model. In our evaluations, no other model gains from passing the group id in any encoding, consistent with the results reported in the BeyondArena paper <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib3" title="" class="ltx_ref">3</a>]</cite>, where only TabPFN-2.6 benefits from
removing the grouped preprocessing for label-per-sample grouped datasets. For each model, we only evaluate whether passing the group id helps for the default model, not the tuned and ensembled version, so we cannot exclude that
these versions would benefit from the group id (though they arguably already use the group-aware validation splits).</p>
</div>
<div id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p3" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p3.1" class="ltx_p">On grouped data without large tables, when the group id is included as input, <span id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p3.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> moves from 1240 to 1288 Elo (rank 6 to rank 1), <span id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p3.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span> from 1139 to 1176, and TabFM from 1167 to 1187. On grouped data
including large tables, <span id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p3.1.3" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> moves from 1187 to 1267 Elo (rank 9 to rank 4) and <span id="A3.SS2.SSS3.p3.1.4" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span> from 1078 to 1108.</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>
<section id="A3.SS3" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">C.3 </span>Additional Results on STRABLE</h3>

<div id="A3.SS3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">To evaluate performance on the 108 datasets of STRABLE (3 folds each), Elo ratings were computed jointly over a total of 200 methods. Specifically, we include only the 196 baselines from the original paper that successfully completed all 324 runs (108 datasets <math id="A3.SS3.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>×</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\times</annotation></semantics></math> 3 folds). On top of these, we added <span id="A3.SS3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> and <span id="A3.SS3.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Fast</span> with TF-IDF, <span id="A3.SS3.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> with <span id="A3.SS3.p1.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">e5-small-v2</span> <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib41" title="" class="ltx_ref">41</a>]</cite>, <span id="A3.SS3.p1.1.5" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Plus</span>, and <span id="A3.SS3.p1.1.6" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Thinking</span>.</p>
</div>
<div id="A3.SS3.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS3.p2.1" class="ltx_p">Figure <a href="#A3.F17" title="Figure 17 ‣ C.3 Additional Results on STRABLE ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">17</span></a> displays a selected list of baselines, focusing specifically on models that are either end-to-end or utilize the stronger string encoding methods. Because the Elo ratings are derived from the comprehensive joint fit over all methods, the values remain identical to those presented in the main paper’s figure, which simply filtered out a few of these pipelines for visual clarity.</p>
</div>
<div id="A3.SS3.p3" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS3.p3.1" class="ltx_p">Alongside our models, the 19 representative published pipelines shown include Ridge <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib54" title="" class="ltx_ref">54</a>]</cite>, ExtraTrees <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib55" title="" class="ltx_ref">55</a>]</cite>, XGBoost <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib56" title="" class="ltx_ref">56</a>]</cite>, RealMLP <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib57" title="" class="ltx_ref">57</a>]</cite>, TabM <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib58" title="" class="ltx_ref">58</a>]</cite>, TabICLv2 <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib32" title="" class="ltx_ref">32</a>]</cite>, and TabPFN-2.5 <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib17" title="" class="ltx_ref">17</a>]</cite>, all utilizing TF-IDF string encodings; four of these also feature variants with <span id="A3.SS3.p3.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">e5-small-v2</span> embeddings. Additionally, we include baselines that process raw text directly: CatBoost <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib39" title="" class="ltx_ref">39</a>]</cite>, ConTextTab <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib36" title="" class="ltx_ref">36</a>]</cite>, and TabSTAR <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib40" title="" class="ltx_ref">40</a>]</cite>.</p>
</div>
<div id="A3.SS3.p4" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS3.p4.1" class="ltx_p">When evaluating pipelines equipped with <span id="A3.SS3.p4.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">e5-small-v2</span> embeddings, the relative rankings remain consistent: <span id="A3.SS3.p4.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> narrowly outperforms TabPFN-2.5, albeit by a smaller margin than observed with TF-IDF. Crucially, <span id="A3.SS3.p4.1.3" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Thinking</span> and <span id="A3.SS3.p4.1.4" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Plus</span>, which process the raw text directly, retain the first and second positions on the overall leaderboard by a substantial margin. Finally, consistent with the official STRABLE benchmark findings, TF-IDF generally proves to be the stronger text representation. This aligns with the nature of the strings in the benchmark, which are mostly short entries rather than long-form free text.</p>
</div>
<figure id="A3.F17" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="2609.17895v2/elo_strable_all.svg" id="A3.F17.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_square" style="aspect-ratio:405/414;" width="405" height="414"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A3.F17.4" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 17</span>: </span><span id="A3.F17.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" style="font-size:90%;">Elo on STRABLE.<span id="A3.F17.5.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_medium"> Elo ratings for a representative subset of methods, derived from a joint fit of all methods across all 108 datasets.</span></span></figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
<section id="A3.SS4" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">C.4 </span>Additional Results on MulTaBench</h3>

<div id="A3.SS4.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS4.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Figure <a href="#A3.F18" title="Figure 18 ‣ C.4 Additional Results on MulTaBench ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">18</span></a> displays the Elo rankings evaluated specifically on the subset of 20 text-focused datasets of MulTaBench (5 folds each). The Elo ratings are computed jointly over the 13 baseline models published in the official benchmark: TabICLv2 <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib32" title="" class="ltx_ref">32</a>]</cite>, TabPFN-2.5 <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib17" title="" class="ltx_ref">17</a>]</cite>, TabPFN-v2 <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib16" title="" class="ltx_ref">16</a>]</cite>, TabM <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib58" title="" class="ltx_ref">58</a>]</cite>, TabDPT <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib59" title="" class="ltx_ref">59</a>]</cite>, CatBoost <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib39" title="" class="ltx_ref">39</a>]</cite>, RealMLP <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib57" title="" class="ltx_ref">57</a>]</cite>, LightGBM <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib60" title="" class="ltx_ref">60</a>]</cite>, XGBoost <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib56" title="" class="ltx_ref">56</a>]</cite>, and Random Forest <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib61" title="" class="ltx_ref">61</a>]</cite>, all of which, like <span id="A3.SS4.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span>, utilize <span id="A3.SS4.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">e5-small-v2</span> <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib41" title="" class="ltx_ref">41</a>]</cite> embeddings, as well as AutoGluon-MM <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib62" title="" class="ltx_ref">62</a>]</cite>, ConTextTab <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib36" title="" class="ltx_ref">36</a>]</cite>, and TabSTAR <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib40" title="" class="ltx_ref">40</a>]</cite>, which process the raw text directly. In addition, we run our own models: <span id="A3.SS4.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span>, <span id="A3.SS4.p1.1.4" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Fast</span>, <span id="A3.SS4.p1.1.5" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Plus</span>, and <span id="A3.SS4.p1.1.6" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Thinking</span>.</p>
</div>
<div id="A3.SS4.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS4.p2.1" class="ltx_p">Focusing exclusively on these text datasets, the performance gap between our models and the baselines widens further compared to the full benchmark. <span id="A3.SS4.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> and <span id="A3.SS4.p2.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Fast</span>, despite relying on the same frozen <span id="A3.SS4.p2.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">e5-small-v2</span> features as the non-native baselines, extend their lead over prior tabular foundation models such as TabPFN-2.5 and TabICLv2. Crucially, <span id="A3.SS4.p2.1.4" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Plus</span> and <span id="A3.SS4.p2.1.5" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Thinking</span> achieve an even greater advantage, securing the first and second position by a substantial margin.</p>
</div>
<figure id="A3.F18" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="2609.17895v2/elo_multabench_text.svg" id="A3.F18.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" style="aspect-ratio:405/312;" width="405" height="312"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A3.F18.5" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 18</span>: </span><span id="A3.F18.6" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" style="font-size:90%;">Elo on MulTaBench text subset.<span id="A3.F18.6.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_medium"> Elo ratings for all evaluated methods, computed jointly across the 20 text-focused datasets with 5 folds each. The <span id="A3.F18.6.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> family dominates the benchmark.</span></span></figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
<section id="A3.SS5" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">C.5 </span>TALENT</h3>

<div id="A3.SS5.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS5.p1.1" class="ltx_p">TALENT <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib63" title="" class="ltx_ref">63</a>]</cite> is a large collection of tabular datasets from many domains. Its base benchmark has 300 datasets spanning classification and regression tasks, and the authors later added extensions with larger and higher-dimensional datasets. Every dataset comes with a fixed split of 64% training, 16% validation and 20% test rows. All methods are ranked on each dataset, and the average rank is reported with a 95% bootstrap confidence interval over datasets. A method that has no result on some datasets gets those cells filled with the score of the KNN baseline; its bar is hatched and its label says how many cells were filled. The <span id="A3.SS5.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span> report <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib1" title="" class="ltx_ref">1</a>]</cite> describes these steps in detail.</p>
</div>
<div id="A3.SS5.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS5.p2.1" class="ltx_p">We show two views of TALENT.</p>
</div>
<section id="A3.SS5.SSS0.Px1" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h5 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">TabICLv2 evaluation protocol.</h5>

<div id="A3.SS5.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS5.SSS0.Px1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><a href="#A3.F19" title="In All TALENT datasets up to one million training rows. ‣ C.5 TALENT ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">19</span></a> follows the evaluation protocol of the TabICLv2 paper <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib32" title="" class="ltx_ref">32</a>]</cite>: the 300 base datasets minus the 26 that were used during the development of TabPFN-2 and TabICLv2, 274 datasets in total, with the baselines the TabICLv2 authors ran. This is the same setup as the main TALENT figure of the <span id="A3.SS5.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span> report, with <span id="A3.SS5.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> added. <span id="A3.SS5.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> ranks first with an average rank of 2.95, ahead of <span id="A3.SS5.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.4" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span> at 4.48 and TabICLv2 at 5.35. The confidence intervals of <span id="A3.SS5.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.5" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> and <span id="A3.SS5.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.6" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span> do not overlap.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="A3.SS5.SSS0.Px2" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h5 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">All TALENT datasets up to one million training rows.</h5>

<div id="A3.SS5.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS5.SSS0.Px2.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><a href="#A3.F20" title="In All TALENT datasets up to one million training rows. ‣ C.5 TALENT ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">20</span></a> uses every TALENT dataset with published baselines and at most one million training rows: the 300 base datasets plus the large-data and high-dimensional extensions, 337 datasets in total. The baselines are the results published by the TALENT authors. The TabICLv2 results used in <a href="#A3.F19" title="In All TALENT datasets up to one million training rows. ‣ C.5 TALENT ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">19</span></a> do not cover the extensions, and the TALENT tables list earlier TabPFN versions only on a small part of the datasets, so this view compares against classical and deep tabular baselines only. <span id="A3.SS5.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> again ranks first, with an average rank of 2.13 against 3.20 for <span id="A3.SS5.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span>.</p>
</div>
<figure id="A3.F19" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="2609.17895v2/tabiclv2-average-rank__20260904-talent-plain-8est-tabpfn8b35121.svg" id="A3.F19.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" style="aspect-ratio:476/180;" width="476" height="180"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A3.F19.8" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 19</span>: </span><span id="A3.F19.9" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" style="font-size:90%;">Average rank on the TALENT benchmark, using the TabICLv2 evaluation protocol <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib32" title="" class="ltx_ref">32</a>]</cite> (274 datasets).<span id="A3.F19.9.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_medium"> Same datasets and baselines as the main TALENT figure of the <span id="A3.F19.9.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span> report <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib1" title="" class="ltx_ref">1</a>]</cite>, with <span id="A3.F19.9.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> added. Lower is better; <span id="A3.F19.9.1.3" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> in plum, <span id="A3.F19.9.1.4" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span> in dark blue. Error bars are 95% bootstrap confidence intervals over datasets. Hatched bars have KNN-imputed cells, counted in the label.</span></span></figcaption>
</figure>
<figure id="A3.F20" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="2609.17895v2/talent-average-rank__20260904-talent-plain-8est-tabpfn8b35121.svg" id="A3.F20.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" style="aspect-ratio:476/228;" width="476" height="228"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A3.F20.6" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 20</span>: </span><span id="A3.F20.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" style="font-size:90%;">Average rank on all TALENT datasets with up to one million training rows (337 datasets).<span id="A3.F20.7.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_medium"> The 300 base datasets plus the large-data and high-dimensional extensions, against the baselines published by the TALENT authors <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib63" title="" class="ltx_ref">63</a>]</cite>. Lower is better; <span id="A3.F20.7.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> in plum, <span id="A3.F20.7.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span> in dark blue. Error bars are 95% bootstrap confidence intervals over datasets. Hatched bars have KNN-imputed cells, counted in the label.</span></span></figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
</section>
<section id="A3.SS6" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">C.6 </span>fev-bench</h3>

<div id="A3.SS6.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS6.p1.1" class="ltx_p">fev-bench <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib7" title="" class="ltx_ref">7</a>]</cite> is a forecasting benchmark of 100 tasks on
public series, scored as skill over Seasonal Naive with the SQL (quantile) and
MASE (point) metrics. We run <span id="A3.SS6.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> through TabPFN-TS <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib18" title="" class="ltx_ref">18</a>]</cite>,
which casts each series as a regression over calendar and seasonal features,
with the inference config of the published TabPFN-TS-3 entry (context 32768, 12
AutoSeasonal periods), and score against the current upstream leaderboard field
of 29 methods. <a href="#A3.F21" title="In C.6 fev-bench ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">21</span></a> shows the result: <span id="A3.SS6.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> places
sixth, 1.1 points above TabPFN-TS-3 at 2.4<math id="A3.SS6.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>×</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\times</annotation></semantics></math> its speed, behind TimesFM-3,
the two Chronos-2 variants, TiRex-2 and Toto-2.0. We emphasize that the <span id="A3.SS6.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> checkpoint is the general
tabular model with no time-series finetuning, whereas TabPFN-TS-3 is a
time-series-specific checkpoint.</p>
</div>
<figure id="A3.F21" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="2609.17895v2/fev-bench-sql-mase-skill-score__20260903-fevbench-ctx32k-k12-upstream-a6f8a7b8.svg" id="A3.F21.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" style="aspect-ratio:476/266;" width="476" height="266"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A3.F21.5" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 21</span>: </span><span id="A3.F21.6" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" style="font-size:90%;">fev-bench.<span id="A3.F21.6.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_medium"> SQL (left) and MASE (right) skill score
relative to Seasonal Naive on the 100 fev-bench tasks. Top 15 of 29 methods;
each panel is sorted on its own metric. </span>TabPFN-TS-3.5 uses the default <span id="A3.F21.6.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> checkpoint<span id="A3.F21.6.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_medium">, whereas TabPFN-TS-3 uses a specialized time-series-specific checkpoint <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib1" title="" class="ltx_ref">1</a>]</cite>. Both are used inside our tabpfn-time-series wrapper.</span></span></figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
<section id="A3.SS7" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">C.7 </span>ScoringBench</h3>

<div id="A3.SS7.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS7.p1.1" class="ltx_p">ScoringBench <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib38" title="" class="ltx_ref">38</a>]</cite> evaluates the full predictive distribution of regression models with proper scoring rules rather than point error. It has 101 OpenML regression datasets, each subsampled to 3,000 rows and scored with 5-fold cross-validation. Its headline metric is the continuous ranked probability score (CRPS); it also reports interval scores, coverage, energy scores and density-based rules. All methods are ranked on each dataset, with folds averaged first, and the average rank over the 101 datasets is reported.</p>
</div>
<div id="A3.SS7.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS7.p2.1" class="ltx_p">We ran <span id="A3.SS7.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> and <span id="A3.SS7.p2.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Fast</span> through the benchmark’s own harness, with their default inference configuration and no tuning on the benchmark. The 51 baselines are the results the ScoringBench authors publish with the benchmark, including <span id="A3.SS7.p2.1.3" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span>, TabICLv2 and EXAONE-Tabular; we did not re-run them. To check that our runs are comparable with these results, we re-ran the released <span id="A3.SS7.p2.1.4" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span> under the same code: it reproduces its published CRPS to within <math id="A3.SS7.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="10^{-3}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">10^{-3}</annotation></semantics></math> relative and lands on the same mean rank.</p>
</div>
<div id="A3.SS7.p3" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS7.p3.1" class="ltx_p"><a href="#A3.F22" title="In C.7 ScoringBench ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">22</span></a> shows the CRPS ranking. <span id="A3.SS7.p3.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> places first with a mean rank of 2.85 and <span id="A3.SS7.p3.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Fast</span> second at 5.76; <span id="A3.SS7.p3.1.3" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> improves on <span id="A3.SS7.p3.1.4" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span> (mean rank 7.64) on 85 of the 101 datasets.</p>
</div>
<figure id="A3.F22" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="2609.17895v2/scoringbench-crps-leaderboard__20260902-scoringbench-f0ed73f.svg" id="A3.F22.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" style="aspect-ratio:476/227;" width="476" height="227"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A3.F22.10" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 22</span>: </span><span id="A3.F22.11" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" style="font-size:90%;">ScoringBench.<span id="A3.F22.11.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_medium"> Left: mean rank on CRPS across the 101 datasets for the top 12 of 53 methods; folds are averaged per dataset, methods ranked within each dataset, and ranks averaged. Lower is better; <span id="A3.F22.11.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> and <span id="A3.F22.11.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Fast</span> in plum. Right: per-dataset relative CRPS improvement of <span id="A3.F22.11.1.3" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> over <span id="A3.F22.11.1.4" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span>, sorted; positive bars are datasets <span id="A3.F22.11.1.5" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> wins (85 of 101, one-sided sign test <math id="A3.F22.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="p=8\times 10^{-13}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mn>8</mn><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>13</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p=8\times 10^{-13}</annotation></semantics></math>, median <math id="A3.F22.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="+1.3\%" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mn>1.3</mn><mo mathsize="1.110em">%</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">+1.3\%</annotation></semantics></math>). Published methods are ScoringBench’s own committed results; <span id="A3.F22.11.1.6" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span> re-run under the same code reproduces its published CRPS to within <math id="A3.F22.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="10^{-3}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">10^{-3}</annotation></semantics></math> relative.</span></span></figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
<section id="A3.SS8" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">C.8 </span>Details on the benchmark overview table</h3>

<div id="A3.SS8.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS8.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><a href="#S0.T1" title="" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Table</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> summarises the seven tabular benchmarks of this report in
one table. This section states how each column is computed.</p>
</div>
<section id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px1" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h5 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Family member.</h5>

<div id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Each benchmark is represented by the best <span id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> family member evaluated
on it: <span id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Thinking</span> on TabArena, BeyondArena, STRABLE and MulTaBench (on STRABLE and MulTaBench
inside <span id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Plus</span>, which handles the raw text columns); TabPFN-Rel (3.5), the internal preview of the
TabPFN-Rel harness <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib6" title="" class="ltx_ref">6</a>]</cite> on <span id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.4" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Plus</span>, on RelArena-<math id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>α</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha</annotation></semantics></math>; and <span id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.5" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> on TALENT and ScoringBench, where
no other family member was evaluated.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px2" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h5 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Rank.</h5>

<div id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">The position of that member on the benchmark’s headline ranking as reported in this
report: Elo on TabArena, BeyondArena, STRABLE, MulTaBench and RelArena-<math id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>α</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha</annotation></semantics></math>, mean rank on TALENT and
ScoringBench. The denominator counts every method on that ranking except the other <span id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5</span> family
members: the 88 entries of the TabArena field, every tuning variant counted separately (<a href="#A3.SS1.SSS2" title="C.1.2 TabArena leaderboard tables ‣ C.1 Additional details on TabArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">C.1.2</span></a>);
the 28 pinned BeyondArena baselines; the 196 published STRABLE pipelines of the joint Elo fit
(<a href="#A3.SS3" title="C.3 Additional Results on STRABLE ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">C.3</span></a>); the 13 published MulTaBench models; the 11 official RelArena-<math id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px2.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>α</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha</annotation></semantics></math> baselines; the
35 TALENT baselines published by the TALENT authors plus <span id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3</span> <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib1" title="" class="ltx_ref">1</a>]</cite> (<a href="#A3.SS5" title="C.5 TALENT ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">C.5</span></a>); and the 51
published ScoringBench methods (<a href="#A3.SS7" title="C.7 ScoringBench ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">C.7</span></a>).</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px3" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h5 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Competitors.</h5>

<div id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">The best other tabular foundation model and the best non-foundation model are the
highest-placed such methods on the same ranking. Systems that contain foundation models (AutoGluon 1.6 <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib14" title="" class="ltx_ref">14</a>]</cite> on
TabArena, AutoGluon-MM <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib62" title="" class="ltx_ref">62</a>]</cite> on MulTaBench) are left out of both columns; <span id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">TabPFN-3.5-Thinking</span> wins 79% of
TabArena splits against AutoGluon 1.6 (noncommercial) and 82% against AutoGluon 1.6 (extreme), and 77%
of MulTaBench splits against AutoGluon-MM. On RelArena-<math id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px3.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>α</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha</annotation></semantics></math> the foundation-model column holds RT-PluRel <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib45" title="" class="ltx_ref">45</a>, <a href="#bib.bib47" title="" class="ltx_ref">47</a>]</cite>, a
Relational Transformer fine-tuned per task from a PluRel-pretrained checkpoint, the closest to a foundation
model in that field, and the non-foundation column holds KurveRSC <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib48" title="" class="ltx_ref">48</a>]</cite>, a GraphReduce <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib64" title="" class="ltx_ref">64</a>]</cite> feature search with
CatBoost <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib39" title="" class="ltx_ref">39</a>]</cite>; both are system entries there. The 337-dataset TALENT view has no other foundation model, so its
TabICLv2 <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">[<a href="#bib.bib32" title="" class="ltx_ref">32</a>]</cite> entry is taken from the 274-dataset TabICLv2-protocol view (<a href="#A3.F19" title="In All TALENT datasets up to one million training rows. ‣ C.5 TALENT ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">19</span></a>).</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px4" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h5 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Win rate.</h5>

<div id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px4.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px4.p1.1" class="ltx_p">As on TabArena: every split (dataset and fold) is compared on its own, a tie counts
as half a win, the per-split results are averaged within a dataset and then over datasets, so every dataset
has the same weight. On RelArena-<math id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px4.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>α</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha</annotation></semantics></math> and TALENT a dataset has one split. TabArena has 51 datasets and 816
splits, BeyondArena 142 and 507 (the core view, group identifier exposed to the TabPFN models,
<a href="#A3.SS2.SSS3" title="C.2.3 Exposing the group identifier on label-per-sample grouped tasks ‣ C.2 Additional results on BeyondArena ‣ Appendix C More Experimental Results" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">C.2.3</span></a>), STRABLE 108 and 324, MulTaBench 40 and 200, RelArena-<math id="A3.SS8.SSS0.Px4.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>α</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha</annotation></semantics></math> 21 tasks,
TALENT 337 datasets and ScoringBench 101 datasets with 5 folds.</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>
</section>
